AGREEMENT FOR REVIEW- REIMBURSEMENT OF
CITY EXPENSES AND PLEDGE AGREEMENT

Agreement made this, abt bay of May 2026 (“Agreement”) between the City of Belvidere,
Illinois, a municipal corporation of the State of Illinois, organized under the laws of the State of
Illinois (“City”) and Datawatt Hyperscale LLC, a Delaware Limited Liability company having its
office at 205 South Peoria Street Unit 1716, Chicago IL 60607 (“Developer”).

RECITALS

A, The Developer proposes to develop the real estate currently lying outside the corporate
limits of the City and referred to as Project Hawkeye (the “Project”), for which the Developer
will or has submitted a proposal or proposals thereof to the City, and for which Project the
Developer will be seeking an Annexation, Pre-Annexation and /or a Development Agreement (the

Agreement); and

B. The City is willing to draft, review, revise and propose language for, and engage in good
faith negotiations with Developer, concerning the development or proposals for the Project and
the Agreement as well as post Agreement City required administration of the Project, provided
Developer reimburses City for its City Expenses, as herein defined, incurred by the City in so
doing from and after April 1, 2026, and as may also be required by City’s Municipal Code.

In consideration of the covenants and agreements herein contained, the sufficiency of
which is acknowledged, it is mutually agreed between the City and the Developer, as follows:

L Upon demand from time to time prior to the approval by the Corporate Authorities
of the Agreement for the Project, the Developer shall reimburse the City for the
following expenses incurred in City’s preparation, negotiation and review from and
after April 1, 2026 that are related to the Project and /or the Agreement

(collectively, the “City Expenses”:

1. The costs incurred by the City for outside engineering services.

2. All attorneys’ fees incurred by the City.

3 All outside (not City employees) planning, financing, financial,
development, environmental and/or geotechnical studies, right of way and
property data and any consultants therefore, or other consultants; and

4, Miscellaneous City out of pocket expenses.

a. The effective date of this Agreement (the “Effective Date’) is the date that the
last of the City and the Developer has executed this Agreement:

I. ‘Upon demand by City, Developer shall from time to time following the approval

by the Corporate Authorities of the Agreement (the “Approved Project”), promptly
reimburse the City for all reasonable City Expenses incurred by City in the

1jPage

---- page 13 ----

administration of the Approved Project including and following the platting
approvals. Such reimbursement shall include engineering expenses, attorneys’ fees,
environmental and/or geotechnical studies, right of way and property data,
consultants' fees, and out of pocket expenses involving various matters such as, but
not limited to, review of the Approved Project, proposals and documents,
preparation and publication, if any, of all notices, resolutions, ordinances and other
documents required under any such arrangement, recording fees, and the review,
negotiation and/or preparation of declarations, covenants, easements, notes, bonds,
letters of credit and escrow agreements or any other documents attributable to the
Approved Project. The Developer’s obligation pursuant to this paragraph IIT shall
not extend to reimbursement of City’s expenses incurred for work or services
provided to City following the issuance of the first certificate of occupancy for the
completion of construction of the initial building consisting of at least 100,000
square feet on the project site unless the City’s expenses are in relation to any of
the following: final plat approval including any documentation or requirement
relating to final plating; required by City Code; or provided for in subsequent
agreement with the Developer.

The City Expenses incurred in the administration of the Project process, the Project,
the Approved Project or any subsequent agreement shall be evidenced to the
Developer by an itemized statement of the City, and in the event that the services
for which the City Expenses were incurred were performed by other than City’s
full-time staff personnel, as 1o those amounts of the City Expenses, shall be
evidenced by the billings therefore and may be further confirmed by the Developer,
at its option, from additional documents designated from time to time by the
Developer, relevant to determining the City Expenses.

In the event the City institutes legal proceedings against the Developer for violation of this
Agreement and secures a judgment in its favor, the court having jurisdiction of the matter
shall determine and include in its judgment against the Developer that it has jurisdiction
over, all expenses of such legal proceedings incurred by City, including but not limited to
the court costs and reasonable attorneys’ fees, witnesses’ fees, etc. incurred by the City in
connection with any such proceedings, Developer may, in its sole discretion, appeal any
judgment rendered in favor of the City against them or any one of them, and shall pay to
the City all the expenses of those appellate legal proceedings or any further appeal thereof,
only after the entry of a final, non- appealable order in favor of the City.

To secure Developer’s performance of this Agreement, but not as a limitation to the
amount of expenses to be reimbursed, Developer’ shall deposit as a pledge with City
$150,000 in cash (the Pledge). Said Pledge amount shall be available for City to draw upon
after 35 days have expired from the date Developer receives a reimbursement billing from
City, and said billing is unpaid. The balance of the Pledge, shall be returned to Developer
within sixty (60) days after issuance of the first certificate of occupancy as set forth in
Section III above or a denial by the City of the requested annexation or annexation /
redevelopment agreement and payment by Developer of all City Expenses incurred under

this Agreement.
This Agreement does not obligate City to continue the process to reach the goal of

2|Page

---- page 14 ----

the Developer if the City in its sole opinion determines that it no longer desires (for
whatever reason or no reason) to continue the process. This Agreement is entered
into by City for the convenience of Developer to enable the City to review and
process Developer’s proposal(s) for the Project. The City agrees to make a good
faith effort to review Developer’s project in connection with this agreement.

In the event of a bankruptcy (whether voluntary or involuntary), of any LLC,
corporation or any other entity, which is or becomes the Developer of the Project,
the undersigned Developer agrees to reimburse the City for any cost or expense
including City’s attomney’s fees in participating in such Bankruptcy proceedings or
pursuing any actions or defenses concerning such insolvency.

Any notice, request, demand, instruction or other document to be given or served
hereunder shall be in writing and shall be delivered personally, or sent by a
nationally recognized overnight express courier, and shall be addressed to the
parties at their respective addresses set forth below, and the same shall be effective
upon receipt if delivered personally, or three (3) business day after deposit with a
nationally recognized overnight express courier. Notices may be sent by the City’s
attorney or by Developer’s attomey, on the City’s or the Developer’s behalf, as
applicable, and such notices sent by the City’s attomey or Developer's attorney, as
applicable, shall be effective notices as if sent directly by the City or the Developer,

as applicable.

Good afternoon Brent,

I hope all is well with you. As we are having some more advanced conversations about this project, | wanted
to see if we could reconvene and discuss water and sewer servicing options in a little more detail. Goals for
this meeting focusing on technical aspects would be to have a good idea of connection points, routes, costs
and incentives. Understanding the incentives might need to be a separate meeting with a larger group.

As an update per your request in our meeting late last year: we are anticipating a primarily air cooled system
within the 250-500 MGD capacity from your current well. There is a small possibility of a water cooled system
which gets into several million gallons per day—! would like to spend a little time on this to understand its

possibility relative to aquifer capacity.

Let me know what your schedule looks like for next week for us to convene in person or on a call.

Thank you,

Kevin

| Caution: Message from external send

Hi Mayor Morris and Mike,

Conor and | are following up on an email he received earlier today from Mayor Morris and wanted to provide
you with an update on Project Hawkeye.

Our apologies for not connecting with you sooner and keeping you apprised of project status since last
speaking with you late last year. Our core focus has been on evaluating the interconnection process with
ComEd and the timing of power delivery to our site. We’ve had multiple conversations with ComEd to assess
timing of submitting a load request application and are hearing of a growing imbalance between the demand
for power and ComEd’s current capacity to supply this power. This imbalance is directly related to the high
volume of load requests from data centers to ComEd and the need for substantial investment in new power
generation and transmission infrastructure to meet this power demand.

Although we anticipated this imbalance in power demand / supply and structured our business model to
address this issue, the pace at which this imbalance has materialized has exceeded even the expectations of
the broader power industry. Just a year ago, ComEd was not signaling the significant shifts now shaping the
energy landscape. ComEd is now suggesting that delivery of power could extend beyond six years from
submittal of load request. This long time horizon makes it difficult to attract end-user data customers who

require immediate access to power.

We have been working hard to find solutions that will bridge this gap in power supply and allow us to develop
and power the site independently of ComEd. We have an unwavering belief in the potential of Project
Hawkeye and are doing everything we can to create power solutions to deliver on this potential.

Conor and | would welcome a call at your convenience to discuss project status in greater detail. Let us know
what works best for you.

Best regards, David

Hi Mayor Morris and Mike,

Conor and | are following up on an email he received earlier today from Mayor Morris and wanted to provide
you with an update on Project Hawkeye.

---- page 217 ----

Our apologies for not connecting with you sooner and keeping you apprised of project status since last
speaking with you late last year. Our core focus has been on evaluating the interconnection process with
ComEd and the timing of power delivery to our site. We’ve had multiple conversations with ComEd to assess
timing of submitting a load request application and are hearing of a growing imbalance between the demand
for power and ComEd’s current capacity to supply this power. This imbalance is directly related to the high
volume of load requests from data centers to ComEd and the need for substantial investment in new power
generation and transmission infrastructure to meet this power demand.

Although we anticipated this imbalance in power demand / supply and structured our business model to
address this issue, the pace at which this imbalance has materialized has exceeded even the expectations of
the broader power industry. Just a year ago, ComEd was not signaling the significant shifts now shaping the
energy landscape. ComEd is now suggesting that delivery of power could extend beyond six years from
submittal of load request. This long time horizon makes it difficult to attract end-user data customers who

require immediate access to power.

We have been working hard to find solutions that will bridge this gap in power supply and allow us to develop
and power the site independently of ComEd. We have an unwavering belief in the potential of Project
Hawkeye and are doing everything we can to create power solutions to deliver on this potential.

Conor and | would welcome a call at your convenience to discuss project status in greater detail. Let us know
what works best for you.

Best regards, David

From: David Halligan &lt;dhalligan@datawattenergy.com&gt;
Sent: Monday, November 25, 2024 12:11 PM

To: Mike Drella

ce: Mullady, Conor

Subject: Re: Project Hawkeye (LL)

Attachments: DWE Project Hawkeye_11.25.24.pdf

This message originated from an External Source. DO NOT click links or open attachments unless you recognize the
sender and know the content is safe.

Hi Mike,
Thank you very much for your call the other week to discuss Project Hawkeye. We appreciate your support.

Datawatt Energy is providing the attached brief outlining our vision of Project Hawkeye and the related benefits to the City
of Belvidere. In summary, the deck details our intended land use, scope of the project, estimates on job creation and the
economic impact for the City of Belvidere and an estimated timeline to complete. Our sincerest intention is to deliver a
project with thoughtful stewardship for the community of Belvidere.

Per your email below, Datawatt Energy would like to explore the utility easements related to the Bush family properties
and the potential costs to extend the utilities to our project site. In addition, we would like to visit about potential incentives

available to the project after annexation into the City.

After you had time to review and share with Mayor Morris, we would be happy to jump on a call at your convenience to
discuss the project in more detail.

If the provided deliverable is sufficient for your needs to set-up a call with the Bush family, we would like to arrange a call
next week Thursday or Friday, if possible.

Best regards and Happy Thanksgiving. David

David Halligan

Datawatt Energy LLC

Mobile: 1-617-513-8893

Email: dhalligan@datawattenergy.com
Web: www.datawattenergy.com

Appreciate the support.
Conor

Conor—

Thank you for taking the time to talk about the project. As we discussed, the City is requesting
additional information regarding the project including, but not limited to, land uses, jobs creation,
economic impact, timeline and scope of the project. The City would also like an idea of what requests
your client has of the City such as our current utility extension discussions as well as any other requests
your client my have. Obviously, we are a bit early in the discussions and | realize any information
provided or requests are somewhat preliminary in nature. However, it will give the administration

something to work with.

Upon receipt of this information, | can approach the Bush family about a meeting with the Hawkeye
team and the City team to discuss annexation of their properties in order to establish contiguity as well

as utility extensions.

From: David Halligan &lt;dhalligan@datawattenergy.com&gt;
Sent: Monday, November 25, 2024 12:11 PM

To: Mike Drella

Cec: Mullady, Conor

Subject: Re: Project Hawkeye (JLL)

Attachments: DWE Project Hawkeye_11.25.24.pdf

This message originated from an External Source. DO NOT click links or open attachments unless you recognize the
sender and know the content is safe.

Hi Mike,
Thank you very much for your call the other week to discuss Project Hawkeye. We appreciate your support.

Datawatt Energy is providing the attached brief outlining our vision of Project Hawkeye and the related benefits to the City
of Belvidere. In summary, the deck details our intended land use, scope of the project, estimates on job creation and the
economic impact for the City of Belvidere and an estimated timeline to complete. Our sincerest intention is to deliver a
project with thoughtful stewardship for the community of Belvidere.

Per your email below, Datawatt Energy would like to explore the utility easements related to the Bush family properties
and the potential costs to extend the utilities to our project site. In addition, we would like to visit about potential incentives

available to the project after annexation into the City.

After you had time to review and share with Mayor Morris, we would be happy to jump on a call at your convenience to
discuss the project in more detail.

If the provided deliverable is sufficient for your needs to set-up a call with the Bush family, we would like to arrange a call
next week Thursday or Friday, if possible.

Best regards and Happy Thanksgiving. David

David Halligan

Datawatt Energy LLC

Mobile: 1-617-513-8893

Email: dhalligan@datawattenergy.com
Web: www.datawattenergy.com

On Nov 19, 2024, at 10:45 AM, Mullady, Conor &lt;Conor.Mullady@jll.com&gt; wrote:
Mike -

Good morning.

morning to further refine our development schedule and timeline.

We are aiming to have this deliverable to you later this week or early next week.

Appreciate the support.
Conor

To: Mullady, Conor &lt;Conor.Mullady @ijll.com&gt;
Subject: RE: Project Hawkeye (JLL)

| Caution: Message from external sender

Conor —

Thank you for taking the time to talk about the project. As we discussed, the City is requesting
additional information regarding the project including, but not limited to, land uses, jobs creation,
economic impact, timeline and scope of the project. The City would also like an idea of what requests
your client has of the City such as our current utility extension discussions as well as any other requests
your client my have. Obviously, we are a bit early in the discussions and | realize any information
provided or requests are somewhat preliminary in nature. However, it will give the administration

something to work with.

Upon receipt of this information, | can approach the Bush family about a meeting with the Hawkeye
team and the City team to discuss annexation of their properties in order to establish contiguity as well

as utility extensions.

Mike Drella

Hi Mayor Morris and Mike,

Conor and | are following up on an email he received earlier today from Mayor Morris and wanted to provide you with
an update on Project Hawkeye.

Our apologies for not connecting with you sooner and keeping you apprised of project status since last speaking with
you late last year. Our core focus has been on evaluating the interconnection process with ComEd and the timing of
power delivery to our site. We’ve had multiple conversations with ComEd to assess timing of submitting a load request
application and are hearing of a growing imbalance between the demand for power and ComEd’s current capacity to
supply this power. This imbalance is directly related to the high volume of load requests from data centers to ComEd
and the need for substantial investment in new power generation and transmission infrastructure to meet this power

demand.

Although we anticipated this imbalance in power demand / supply and structured our business model to address this
issue, the pace at which this imbalance has materialized has exceeded even the expectations of the broader power
industry. Just a year ago, ComEd was not signaling the significant shifts now shaping the energy landscape. ComEd is
now suggesting that delivery of power could extend beyond six years from submittal of load request. This long time
horizon makes it difficult to attract end-user data customers who require immediate access to power.

We have been working hard to find solutions that will bridge this gap in power supply and allow us to develop and
power the site independently of ComEd. We have an unwavering belief in the potential of Project Hawkeye and are
doing everything we can to create power solutions to deliver on this potential.

Conor and | would welcome a call at your convenience to discuss project status in greater detail. Let us know what works
best for you.

Best regards, David

David Halligan
